ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00167_d7e4519	Federal Minister, please.

The starting point is: The budget is essential for the financing of the common agricultural policy. This is the only area to be shortened, according to the European Commission. We're against it! We want to continue the path of sustainable organic farming. However, this will be very hard negotiations, because the budgetary situation in Europe is currently a difficult one. But we have already achieved a lot, namely that there will be a mining project and that there will be no single European premium. Finally, we have achieved that not only organic farmers, but also farmers participating in an environmental programme, automatically fulfil greening, i.e. the European level's ecologicalisation measures, which is important for Austrian agriculture, because in Austria a large number of farmers participate in the environmental programme, which has led to our being an environmental model country. The timetable is such that at the end of this year the budget of the European Union stands, and then the common agricultural policy is to be fulfilled in detail. It has been agreed to conclude, so to speak, the reform of the common agricultural policy at the European level in spring 2013.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00167_d7e4607	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00167_d7e4623	In principle, we support the European Commission's idea of making agricultural policy more organic, i.e. greening. Austria is strictly opposed to this. But there is also the vast majority, almost all European Member States are against it. It increases the world population, we need more food, and therefore we do not want to shut down areas. However, we are very much in favour of including ecologically valuable areas, nature reserves, shore-accompanied strips in this programme. The ball, however, lies with the European Commission. It wants such a formulation, and therefore the European Commission must also say exactly what it is proposing. And then we have a basis that we can discuss. Austria is willing to include nature reserves, ecological priority areas in the programme, but we do not want to shut down areas because we want a producing agriculture that provides the population with quality food.

The European Union has approved certain genetically modified varieties for cultivation. Austria has established all these varieties with a ban on cultivation, only that can be lifted at any time at European level. Therefore, in 2009, I started the initiative: Right of self-determination on the issue of genetic engineering. In Austria, we want to determine whether we use genetic engineering or not. It's clear we don't want to use genetic engineering. When I started this initiative at the European level, only our Eastern European neighbours supported us. In the meantime, the European Commission has adopted the Austrian idea and says, yes, we want this freedom of genetic engineering, we want states to have the right to declare themselves free from genetic engineering. Meanwhile, a larger number of countries, 22 EU countries, Austria, but the large countries do not support. The major European countries such as Germany, France, Great Britain and Spain do not support this. But we are fighting to ensure that genetic engineering is free. This is a way for Austrian agriculture, and consumers also want it.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00167_d7e5207	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00167_d7e5222	Ladies and gentlemen, I do not share the accusations raised by some people that farmers do not pay taxes. That's not true at all! What the farmers have is that there is a flat-rate tax collection system based on unit values. It's important to hold that. The large farms from a certain level are accountable, fully accountable, and pay taxes according to yield, and the small farms are covered by a flat-rate system, which makes sense. Because if every single farmer had to make an accountancy, every farmer would have a controller behind each farmer, and that would mean an expansion of the bureaucracy. The flat-rate tax collection system makes sense because it is efficient. And you have to say that the incomes in agriculture are low. 2.5 million Austrians do not pay income tax, workers, employees, even farmers, because income is not so high. It is therefore a question of not talking badly about the system, but of modernising a non-bureaucratic system, which ultimately also uses the state.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00167_d7e6815	It is clear, ladies and gentlemen, that it is the top goal to preserve drinking water cleanly. We're successful, too. In Austria, people are supplied with drinking water from spring water and groundwater. This is the top priority, the maintenance of drinking water, and it must be subordinated to everything: agriculture, industry, industry, every individual citizen. It's right what you say: Double promotions must not take place. Under the Public Health Directive, benefits already paid from other public funds are not eligible under the Public Health Directive. That is to say, they are the same benefits. What you are addressing is that there is the ÖPUL programme, but there are also additional measures under contract water protection, additional services that are supported. For example, I should like to remind you of greening under the environmental programme, where it is the goal of preventing, so to speak, a black slab, i.e. naked arable land, and that this is greened so that it does not result in excess of nutrients, or additional fertiliser restrictions on maize cultivation. So the point is: If additional services are provided, they will also be paid for under contract nature protection and water protection.

It makes a difference whether I operate as a farmer in a water area or outside. There the farmers have additional conditions, which means that they are allowed to fertilize less, use less plant protection and also have to work more intensively. ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00167_d7e8817	Madam President. Minister! High house! The new Medical Assistance Act meets the new health care requirements in the draft, with an exception. The law implements several objectives, such as the creation of new requirements and market-oriented areas of activity, the facilitation and facilitation of multiple qualifications, the permeability of training and careers, the increase in working life, the increase in professional opportunities, quality-secured training and the use of existing infrastructures. The scope of the activity was limited exclusively to simple relief services and hand-outs in the previous health services as a number of occupations. Those who now practice medical assistants can be widely used through well-founded training. These assistants are ordination assistants, operating assistants, proselytes, disinfection assistants and plaster assistants. As new medical assistants, laboratory assistance and X-ray assistance are to be created. While we have no problems with laboratory assistance, like all other parties, we are very likely to have our problems with the X-ray assistance task defined in the draft. The X-ray assistance is intended to be able to conduct independent MR and CT examinations, and this is strongly opposed. These studies, carried out so far by radiologists, require great expertise. The radiotechnologist has a three-year high-school or academic education to be very familiar with the medical and technical aspects of MR and CT. This is absolutely necessary in order not to endanger the health of patients during the studies. I don't need to mention what damage caused improperly applied radiation. For this reason, the implementation of MR and CT is exclusively in hand audited radiologists. That is why the four-party amendment was tabled, which ensures this and makes the MAB law a good law.

Young people in particular are often too aware of the risks involved, and unfortunately there are too few doctors who are willing to intervene only after completion of physical development. Unfortunately, many of God’s people are ready to do so too. We have already been pointed out today that such interventions often result in serious complications. It is clear that a beauty surgery is a surgical procedure. The rules to this effect have so far been rather inadequate. That is why there is the law we are now dealing with. It serves to protect health and physical integrity, especially of minors. The new law regulates the medical qualification, it forbids confusing professional designations. It norms the obligation to inform about the intervention itself, but also about the costs. It forbids unindicated aesthetic surgery to under 16-year-olds and grants special protection to 16- to 18-year-olds, as we have already heard: Waiting period of four weeks, consent of the legal guardian or also a psychological consultation before the procedure. This law is a good one; it will take effect next year. I hope that the debate on the law will also lead to the increase in awareness, especially of those who are prepared for such interventions, that it is unique, and that they will also become more aware of the fact that unique people are destroyed by such interventions, often with dramatic results and very high costs.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00167_d7e11608	Mr President. Minister! I would like to greet Dr. Sonja Huber-Wütschitz and Dr. Martina Glatzl in advance. These are the Vice-Presidents of the Austrian Veterinary Chamber who follow our remarks here. Dear colleague Belakovich-Jenewein, if you here claim that 80 percent of all veterinarians are against this law, I must say: It is basically wrong what you say here, because these 80 percent come together only by weighting 27 representatives in a body! That's not 80 percent of all veterinarians! And these 27 representatives did not all vote against this law, but they were actually only 60 percent! [[Abg. Dr. Belakovich-Jenewein: I know you have a problem with democracy!]] Dear colleague Belakovich-Jenewein, now it's not even 50 percent! The veterinarians in Austria say that they are glad that this law is coming. And it is really a good bill presented here by Minister Stöger. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] [[New interlocutory call from Dr Belakovich-Jenewein.]] One who will still tell us in this House is Mr Spadiut, who is a veterinarian himself. He will tell us – and he has already said it in the Committee – what is really right, that it is important to make this law. But, ladies and gentlemen, let us look at the background and the starting point of this law. We know that since September 2002 the Austrian Veterinary Chamber has become a federal unit chamber. It consisted of nine national chambers and a Federal Chamber for Veterinary Doctors. And the current regulations in the Veterinary Law are, in part, even unconstitutional, legislative and partly contradictory. It was therefore necessary to make a new law here, and it is good that a new law is actually coming, which gives the veterinarians and the board legal certainty in all the measures they put in place. What are the essential objectives and amendments to this Veterinary Act? By creating departments for self-employed veterinarians and those who practice their profession in the context of an employment relationship, a restructuring of the Veterinary Chamber is taking place. Of the 2,700 veterinarians we have in Austria, only 650 are self-employed. They are also members of the Veterinary Chamber, but seem to have had no right of representation in these bodies so far. That too is now being changed. Let us see how the work of the veterinarians has changed. – For the most part, women who have recently entered this metier are women, and the majority of them have been transferred from individual care by veterinarians in the agricultural sector to practical groups where there are many employees. It is therefore necessary to put in place appropriate measures here. One of these measures is the adaptation of the right to vote. There will also be a clear regulation of the functions of the institutions and of the board. And there will be a clear presentation of its own and transferred scope of action as well as an improved presentation of the law on direction and supervision. Further changes are: more structured regulation of welfare institutions (funds) and clarification of the tasks of the board of trustees as well as new regulation of the veterinary disciplinary procedure. In addition, this law establishes conciliation bodies and introduces a control committee. Furthermore, the possibility of decision-making is provided for in a special examination of the conduct. And paragraph 2 provides the possibility that the delegates' assembly may recall the bodies it has chosen by a request for distrust. Following a further call, the President of the State Offices will be assisted by a State Committee for Consultation. We have also taken into account the demands made by the veterinarians. One thing I must say, ladies and gentlemen: We have never had such intense discussions with the recipients of a legislative matter in this House as with the veterinarians. We have also made changes in the way we tabled an amendment. I mean, that's a good, excellent law. I therefore ask you to agree to this because I believe that this will help the veterinarians in Austria.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00167_d7e11693	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen – especially you, who are paying attention to us today with your visit! . I am glad that we have discussed properly, perhaps a bit of a disputed case by case, because I think that what is in place here today is a tangible government proposal. Life is characterized by changes, no question, not everything does us well, not everything fits us. The veterinary industry – in a size, as Mr Keck has already said, of about 3,000 members – is also facing this backdrop of change and major challenges. The professional image of the veterinarian has become different. In ancient times it was the wholesale veterinarian. Today we have other forms: Small-scale care, supervision, and we also have the situation that there is not always the veterinarian who is self-employed, but that several employees are active in this field. And to take due account of it. In 2001, there was an initial survey, then one was dissatisfied with the law in force. A Veterinary Chamber Act was then created in 2002, which contained in particular the abolition of the Chambers of State as such. Perhaps at this time one has not yet been able to look at what has actually opened up through this recast. What we are going to advise and decide today is nothing but the re-improvement of the 2002 law. It is about organ functions, it is about Rules of Procedure, it is about the electoral model – all these topics are known to all those who are concerned with this matter. The aim is to restructure the chamber organisation, including through clear allocations of tasks. The aim is also to have national offices carrying out certain tasks there, and to have departments. It is essential to me that we have been deprived of the right to vote in majority voting and have found a right to vote in relation. There are now the Delegates' Assembly with 27 delegates. There are the new national office presidents inside. There are 14 mandates in the Self-Employed Division Committee. And there's a department committee for self-employed people. This is the area of veterinary workers in an employee relationship. And the special thing was that the Veterinary Chamber as such did not have a collective contractual sovereignty, and that is now being regulated accordingly. That is, to sum up, the reorientation is such that there is a very clear structure in the body: the delegation assembly, the department committee. the Board of Directors, the President, a control committee, a board of trustees and the national office presidents. Well, what does the Delegate Assembly have to do as such? – That was certainly a central point in the discussion. It was once to clarify the question: Can a board be selected: yes or no? In old law, that was a question of dispute. It is now clearly regulated: The Delegate Assembly will complete the accounts and discharge of the Executive Board. It is also a matter of establishing the chamber environment. It is also a question of determining the contribution to the welfare facility. I mean, this is a very, very important question, because of the fact that the supply is derived in a corresponding way after professional life or after fate. This is an important question, above all, because the welfare fund has to be dealt with responsibly. The funds are the Members' resources as such. It is also about the issue of the election and appointment of the Board of Directors – I have already said that – and also about the need for the Management Assembly to be elected as such and having a written reporting obligation to the Executive Board. That was worth a lot to us. There are, of course, also conciliation bodies, not a question, because dispute proceedings are also possible among veterinarians, and so that you do not have to call the highest courts, there is now a standard conciliation body. I think this whole development is clear and positive. I am also very glad that we have found a way about the national offices. This is not a separate department as such, but it is an institution which is intended, above all, to implement national legislation and also to refer to this reference in the collective bargaining and in the organization, for example in the areas of animal and animal health, animal welfare, meat inspection, in micro-carcases in domestic slaughters and the like. All of this is now properly regulated. I think this law is a solid basis for preserving and perceiving the interests of the state and the state in the framework of the self-management of the Veterinary Chamber. I am glad, as I said before, that we have had such a broad discussion, and I also thank all those who have understood us. We didn't want to go the next best way, but we really wanted to go the best way, although we knew that of course we won't get the applause of everyone, but that's how it is in life. But there was much understanding and recognition from the representatives of the Veterinary Chamber. Mr. Minister, that wasn't a simple thing! I've already said here, I usually do a bit economical with praise, but here I have to confess, no, out of conviction say: That was difficult, and that has been done! The Minister can be satisfied. However, I can also say that Mr. Dr. Herzog was an important person in the Minister's office who was involved in the development and design of this bill. This must be said, too, in the Minutes of Parliament. He deserved it. Finally, I would also like to thank the representatives of the Veterinary Chamber and wish you, ladies and gentlemen, a great deal of success with the new law.

In the field of work, more than half are women. This would not be a problem of knowledge and ability, only do women have a different access to the profession than men. This situation will now be taken into account and the delegates' assembly reassessed, consisting of political representatives, a department for veterinary doctors working on a professional basis and a department for veterinarians employed in the veterinary profession. The number of representatives is democratically divided. This ensures that the interests of all veterinarians are represented. Another important change is that the Austrian Veterinary Chamber receives the possibility of collective contractual capacity. This is important in so far as it is not rare for young colleagues to be employed around the clock for a hunger wage at professional veterinarians. Mr President. ==References== In April 2012, the agreement between the Republic of Austria and the Swiss Confederation on cooperation in the fields of taxation and financial market was concluded in Bern. This agreement essentially follows those two agreements which Switzerland has also concluded with Germany and UK. In the United Arab Emirates, only Emiratic nationals are admitted as lawyers. That is why Mr Kawadri cannot, in principle, appear himself in court, but needs, if necessary, the special authority of an approved attorney. However, because of his status as legal consultant, Mr Kawadri can work at police stations or in the preliminary proceedings. Therefore, he was able to successfully support Austrian police officers in the Emirates, particularly in police custody, in the framework of police pre-surveillance proceedings. To this end, he was – as mentioned – entitled or accepted by the local authorities on the basis of his profession as a legal consultant. In individual cases, at the request of parties, he was issued so-called No Objection Letters. It turned out, however, that this was also a case of forgery. Mr Kawadri was never made known at any time as a trust attorney of the message. The message has its own trust law firm based in Dubai and a branch office in Abu Dhabi, which is also recommended as such. Since the accusations against Mr Kawadri have been made, the following steps have been taken by the Ministry of Foreign Affairs: The message was ordered to discontinue any cooperation with Mr Kawadri until the final clarification of the allegations. The Embassy was also instructed to inform the United Arab Emirates Ministry of Foreign Affairs of the circulation of documents attributed to the Austrian Embassy and to make appropriate further statements. Consul Dedic was called up in the meantime and has now taken up his service at the Ministry's headquarters. The project report was transmitted to the Federal Ministry of Justice by means of the Supreme Public Prosecutor's Office. To conclude, the Federal Ministry of European and International Affairs immediately after compacting the accusations all the necessary has taken steps to clarify the accusations in the room. Thank you.

The location of the new financial centre in Vienna Mitte is the result of a multi-annual development and planning process. The aim was to create suitable office space, which will enable the requirements for dynamic work processes and working worlds in public administration to be optimally linked to the necessary infrastructural conditions. No commission payments by the BMF were linked to the creation of the contract. With the novelle of capital market law, stock market law and co on the one hand we make a lot of technical changes, but above all it seems politically relevant to me that we change § 91 in the Stock Exchange Act. This means that we are improving the regulation as regards the threshold, as from when it will have to be reported in order to prevent an unmarked appeal to stock companies in the future. We set this threshold of future share acquisition by 4 percent. So everything from 4 percent will be required to report, and in addition, it is provided that a 3 percent reporting obligation may also be included in the statutes. If you look at France, you can see that 95 percent of all stock companies use this voluntary lower reporting threshold. I think that's a step in the right direction. I think that, after some time and some experience, we could take a next step and adapt our thresholds to those of the neighbouring countries, which are on average between 2 and 3 percent. I also find it relevant that in future all the different derivative instruments will be gathered for this reporting obligation. So also, for example, convertible bonds or similar things fall below in the future. And in the event of violations of the obligation to report, the right to vote rests for six months. I believe that this innovation is in any case intended to increase transparency in the capital market, and I welcome the very broad agreement of almost all parties. – Thank you very much.

A tolerant, a humane, a worldly society like Austria must always offer itself as a dialogue partner. Wherever there is a hope for reconciliation, justice, human rights and peace, Austria will be a place to promote this dialogue. There is no alternative to dialogue and therefore the SPÖ supports this agreement.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-06-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00167_d7e21721	We reach the 25th. Agenda item. As this draft law has already been adopted at second reading, we shall now come to the third reading. In accordance with Article 180 of the Rules of Procedure, the third reading shall take place at the earliest 24 hours after the conclusion of the second reading. The second reading was held on 4 October. ==References====External links== Thus, the 24-hour deadline is fulfilled. The present draft law may be amended in accordance with § 82 para. 2 C 2 of the Rules of Procedure shall be decided only on the presence of at least half of the Members and by a majority of two-thirds of the votes cast. I therefore begin by stating the presence of the intended number of Members required for the vote. I shall now vote on the draft law, title and receipt in 1879. I ask those ladies and gentlemen who agree to this draft law at third reading to give a similar sign. - That's the majority. This draft law is therefore also adopted at third reading. I expressly note the two-thirds majority required by constitution. The agenda is exhausted. [[19:31:04]] Reading of a part of the Official Protocol
